\bm{x}_{D}=g^{E_{11}}_{A_{2}}\bm{x}_{A}=\frac{1}{2}(-\bm{T}^{E_{11}}_{A_{1}}+\bm{T}^{E_{11}}_{A_{2}})
.

2.

Origin-rooted FD.

•

Base point:

\bm{x}_{A}\equiv\bm{0}
;

•

Three other corners of the face:

–

\bm{x}_{B}=g^{E_{11}}_{A_{1}}\bm{x}_{A}=\bm{T}^{E_{11}}_{A_{1}}
;

–

\bm{x}_{C}=g^{E_{11}}_{A_{2}}g^{E_{11}}_{A_{1}}\bm{x}_{A}=\bm{T}^{E_{11}}_{A_{1}}+\bm{T}^{E_{11}}_{A_{2}}
;

–

\bm{x}_{D}=g^{E_{11}}_{A_{2}}\bm{x}_{A}=\bm{T}^{E_{11}}_{A_{2}}
.

3.8
E_{12}
: Chimney space with half turn

The chimney space with half turn is a root of
E_{11}
and can be thought of as
E_{2}
with one non-compact dimension.

Properties
:
As listed in
Table

1
, this manifold has compact cross-sections and is orientable, inhomogeneous, and anisotropic.

Generators
:
Similar to
E_{11}
, there are two generators, and similar to
E_{2}
, one of the matrices is a rotation by
\pi
.
Conventionally this rotation is chosen to be around the
y
-axis instead of being around the
z
-axis, as is done in
E_{2}
.
In general (see
Section

A.8
), the generators of
E_{12}
can be written as

\displaystyle\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{12}}_{A}=\mathds{1},\quad\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{12}}_{B}=\bm{\mathsf{R}}_{\bm{\hat{y}}}(\pi)=\diag(-1,1,-1),\quad\mbox{with}

\displaystyle\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{A_{1}}=\begin{pmatrix}L_{Ax}\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix}\equiv\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{1},\quad\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}=\begin{pmatrix}L_{Bx}\\
L_{By}\\
L_{Bz}\end{pmatrix},

(3.39)

or alternately,

\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{A}=L_{A}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}=L_{B}\begin{pmatrix}\cos\beta\sin\gamma\\
\sin\beta\\
\cos\beta\cos\gamma\end{pmatrix}.

(3.40)

Associated
E_{11}
: In addition to
\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{A}
defined above, a second independent translation is

(g^{E_{12}}_{B})^{2}:\bm{x}\to\bm{x}+\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{2},

(3.41)

for

\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{2}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}0\\
2L_{By}\\
0\end{pmatrix}=2L_{B}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sin\beta\\
0\end{pmatrix}\,.

(3.42)

Cross-sectional area
:

A_{E_{12}}=\frac{1}{2}|\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{1}\times\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{2}|=L_{A}L_{B}|\sin\beta|.

(3.43)

Tilts versus origin position
: A shift of origin will change
L_{Bx}
and
L_{Bz}
(the two components of
\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}
perpendicular to the axis of rotation) but will not affect
L_{By}
.
By special choice of origin on the axis of rotation, the tilt can be traded for a shift and we can choose
\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}=L_{B}\bm{\hat{e}}_{y}
at the expense of the observer no longer being at the origin.

Real parameters (4 independent)
: There are 4 independent parameters required to fully define
E_{12}
with some being redundant with shifting the origin.
Thus we have:

•

L_{A}
and
L_{A}/L_{B}
are intrinsic parameters of the manifold;

•

L_{Bx}
and
L_{Bz}
, or equivalently
\beta
and
\gamma
, can be traded for
x^{E_{12}}_{0x}
and
x^{E_{12}}_{0z}
;

•

the standard (special origin, i.e., “untilted”) form is
0=L_{Bx}=L_{Bz}
, or equivalently,
\beta=\pi/2
(with
\gamma
irrelevant).

Parameter ranges
: We want to ensure that we do not double-count parameter choices that appear different but actually generate the same lattice of clones.
Similar to
E_{11}
, we therefore require:

1.

0<L_{A}
and
0<L_{By}
, i.e., choice of orientation;

2.

the lengths of
\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{A}
and
\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}
are unconstrained;

3.

|\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{2}(\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{1})^{2}
, i.e.,
\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}
cannot be shortened by adding or subtracting
\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{1}
.

In terms of the parameters, the necessary conditions become:

1.

0<\beta\leq\pi/2
so that
0<\sin\beta\leq 1
;

3.

|L_{Bx}|\leq\frac{1}{2}L_{Ax}
, or equivalently,
\cos\beta|\cos\gamma|\leq\frac{1}{2}L_{A}/L_{B}
.

3.9
E_{16}
: Slab space including rotation

The slab space
E_{16}
is the basis for all Euclidean three-manifolds with one compact dimension (i.e., compact lengths), in much the same way as
E_{1}
and
E_{11}
are for all compact and two compact dimensions, respectively.
The possibility of having a corkscrew (as opposed to a pure translation) in the slab space appears to be new, at least in the cosmology literature.
While topologically the corkscrew is continuously deformable to the unrotated slab space, physically the corkscrew leads to a distinguishable pattern of clones and the rotation must be by an angle that is a rational multiple of
2\pi
.
Due to this, we split the description of
E_{16}
into two cases:
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
and
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
.

3.9.1
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
: Conventional unrotated slab space

The conventional definition of
E_{16}
only includes a translation.
Here we call this choice
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
.

Properties:
As listed in
Table

1
, this manifold has a compact length and is orientable, homogeneous, and anisotropic.

Generators
: In general, since
E_{16}
has one compact dimension it is described by one generator, which we may take to be a translation in the
z
-direction (see
Section

A.9.1
), so the generator of
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
is

\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}}_{A}=\mathds{1},\quad\mbox{with}\quad\bm{T}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}}_{A}=L{(0,0,1)}^{T}.

(3.44)

Even though there is only one generator, since this generator is a pure translation, we follow the convention of using
A
to label it.

Length
: Since the slab spaces have only one compact dimension their volumes and cross-sectional areas are infinite.
The shortest path length around the manifold at any point is
L
.

Origin
: Since
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}}_{A}=\mathds{1}
the manifold is homogeneous and the lattice of an observer is independent of the location of the observer.

Real parameters (1 independent)
: There is 1 independent parameter required to fully define
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
: the length of the compact dimension.

Parameter ranges
: We want to ensure that we do not double-count parameter choices that appear different but actually generate the same lattice of clones.
In this case, we can always choose
0<L
through the orientation of the coordinate axes.

3.9.2
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
: General rotated slab space

The orientable slab space also allows for a corkscrew motion.
Physically this corkscrew is distinguishable and must be treated as a separate case. As discussed in
Section

A.9.2
, the rotation angle must be a rational multiple of
2\pi
in order for the eigenmodes of the Laplacian not to have azimuthal symmetry around the corkscrew axis.
Such a symmetry would exclude them as a basis for general smooth functions on the manifold.

Properties
: Due to the corkscrew motion this differs from
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
in that it is inhomogeneous.
As listed in
Table

1
, this manifold has a compact length and is orientable, inhomogeneous, and anisotropic.

Generators
: Similar to
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
, there is one generator.
In general the generator of
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
(see
Section

A.9.2
) can be written as

\displaystyle\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B}=\bm{\mathsf{R}}_{\bm{\hat{z}}}(2\pi p/q)=\begin{pmatrix}\cos(2\pi p/q)&-\sin(2\pi p/q)&0\\
\sin(2\pi p/q)&\hphantom{-}\cos(2\pi p/q)&0\\
0&\hphantom{-}0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\mbox{with}

\displaystyle\bm{T}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B}={(L_{x},0,L_{z})}^{T}=L{(\cos\beta,0,\sin\beta)}^{T},

(3.45)

and
p\in\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
,
q\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}
, and
|p|
and
q
relatively prime.
As in
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
, here since the generator is a rotation we use
B
to label it.

Associated
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
: A pure translation can be defined for
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
as

g^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{1}\equiv(g^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B})^{q}:\bm{x}\to\bm{x}+\bm{T}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{1},\quad\mbox{for }\bm{T}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{1}\equiv{(0,0,qL_{z})}^{T}=qL\sin\beta{(0,0,1)}^{T}.

(3.46)

Length
: The length of the associated
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
is

L_{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}=qL|\sin\beta|.

(3.47)

Tilts versus origin position
: A shift of origin will change
L_{x}
and
L_{y}
.
Rotational freedom can always be used to restore
L_{y}=0
.
By special choice of origin on the axis of rotation, the tilt can be traded for a shift and we can choose
\bm{T}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B}=L\bm{\hat{e}}_{z}
at the expense of the observer no longer being at the origin.

Real parameters (2 independent)
: There are 2 independent parameters required to fully define
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
with 1 parameter interchangeable with a shift of origin.
Thus we have:

•

L
is an intrinsic parameter of the manifold;

•

L_{x}
, or equivalently
\beta
, can be traded for
x^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{0x}
;

•

L_{y}=0
is not a parameter, even though it can be traded for
x^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{0y}
; it is redundant with the orientation of the coordinate system, and thus the rotation of an observer’s coordinate system, about the topology rotation axis (
\bm{\hat{e}}_{z}
);

•

the standard (special origin, i.e., “untilted”) form is
0=L_{x}
, or equivalently
\beta=\pi/2
.

Parameter ranges
: We want to ensure that we do not double-count parameter choices that appear different but actually generate the same lattice of clones. Similar to
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
, we can always require
0<L_{x}
and
0<L_{z}
, or equivalently
0<\beta\leq\pi/2
, through orientation of the coordinate system.

3.10
E_{18}
: The covering space,
E^{3}

Three-dimensional Euclidean space
E^{3}
is the covering space.
It is infinite in all directions and has no generators.
As listed in
Table

1
, it has no compact dimensions and is orientable, homogeneous, and the only Euclidean topology that is isotropic.

4
Eigenmodes of the scalar Laplacian and correlation matrices

A key ingredient of cosmological perturbation theory is the set of the scalar (and tensor) eigenmodes of the Laplacian.
Characteristically, it is the amplitudes of these modes for which theories give statistical predictions
[
77
,
78
]
.

In this section, we present the scalar eigenmodes for the orientable Euclidean manifolds in their full generality.
While such eigenmodes have been presented before
[
58
,
59
,
77
,
24
,
60
]
, it has been in a context where the full topology parameter space has not been included, even when its existence has been hinted at. Note that we are not faithful to the notational conventions of those works, so any comparisons should be made carefully.

In the covering space,
E_{18}
, the eigenmodes of the Laplacian are
5
5

5

There are many conventions for normalizing the eigenmodes.
Here we choose not to include any additional factors and will discuss the implications of this for each manifold below.

\Upsilon^{E_{18}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}.

(4.1)

Here
\bm{x}_{0}
is the position of an arbitrary origin relative to the observer’s coordinate system
6
6

6

The inclusion of
\bm{x}_{0}
has no particular role for the covering space, for
E_{1}
, for
E_{11}
, or for
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
, but is crucial
for
E_{2}
–
E_{6}
,
E_{12}
, and
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
.

and
\bm{k}={(k_{x},k_{y},k_{z})}^{T}
, referred to as the wavevector, is any triplet of real numbers with its magnitude
k
referred to as the wavenumber.
Since

\nabla^{2}\Upsilon^{E_{18}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})=-|\bm{k}|^{2}\Upsilon^{E_{18}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x}),

(4.2)

the eigenvalue associated with
\Upsilon^{E_{18}}_{\bm{k}}
is
-|\bm{k}|^{2}\equiv-k^{2}
.
It can assume any non-positive real value.

In standard inflationary cosmological theory, the adiabatic curvature perturbation field
\delta^{\mathcal{R}}
is the sum of the eigenmodes
\Upsilon^{E_{18}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})
with amplitudes
\delta^{\mathcal{R}}(\bm{k})
that are described by Gaussian random variables of zero mean and dimensionless power spectrum
{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)
7
7

7

Some small amount of non-Gaussianity is often predicted, but we reserve such considerations in a topological context for future work.

.
We can write the resulting three-dimensional scalar field as

\delta^{\mathcal{R}}(\bm{x})=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\;\delta^{\mathcal{R}}(\bm{k})\Upsilon^{E_{18}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})\,.

(4.3)

However, we will be interested in other scalar fields
\delta^{X}
that are linearly related to
\delta^{\mathcal{R}}

by a transfer function that we should write as
\Delta^{\mathcal{R}X}
, but we will drop the
\mathcal{R}
.
The expectation value of any pair of
\delta^{X}(\bm{k})
is

C^{E_{18};XY}_{\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\prime}}\equiv\langle\delta^{X}(\bm{k})\delta^{Y*}(\bm{k}^{\prime})\rangle=(2\pi)^{3}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)\Delta^{X}(k)\Delta^{Y*}(k^{\prime})\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime})\;,

(4.4)

where
\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime})
is the three-dimensional Dirac delta function and we have assumed that the transfer function depends only on the magnitude of
\bm{k}
.
8
8

8

For three-dimensional scalar quantities
X
and
Y
the transfer function will typically depend only on the magnitude of
\bm{k}
. However, it may be useful to consider a more general dependence on
\bm{k}
, for example when deriving a transfer function of CMB temperature and polarization on the sky. We will continue to write
\Delta^{X}(k)
.

For the adiabatic curvature
\delta^{\mathcal{R}}
, the primordial power spectrum is often written as
9
9

9

The normalization by
{2\pi^{2}}/{k^{3}}
in (
4.4
) is a common convention, but not universal. In this convention,
{\mathcal{P}}^{X}(k)
is the contribution to the variance per logarithmic interval of wavenumber: the total variance of
\delta^{X}
is
\int\mathrm{d}(\ln k)\;{\mathcal{P}}^{X}(k)
. In the large-scale structure literature, the matter power spectrum is usually denoted by the quantity
P(k)=2\pi^{2}{\mathcal{P}}(k)/k^{3}
.

{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)=A_{\mathrm{s}}\left(\frac{k}{k_{*}}\right)^{n_{\mathrm{s}}-1}\;,

(4.5)

with the scalar amplitude
A_{\mathrm{s}}
defined at the fiducial wavenumber
k_{*}
, and the scalar spectral tilt
n_{\mathrm{s}}
.
We assume throughout that
{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}
is the same function for
E_{1}
–
E_{18}
,
as might be expected to result, for example, from an epoch of inflation, and so do not add a topology label to
{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}
.

Since Euclidean geometry has both translational and rotational isometry, there are other natural bases of the eigenmodes of the Laplacian.
Given the nature of cosmological observations, in particular those of the cosmic microwave background, it is more convenient to work in spherical coordinates
(r,\theta,\phi)
with the plane waves expanded in terms of spherical harmonics as

e^{i\bm{k}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}=4\pi e^{-i\bm{k}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}\sum_{\ell m}i^{\ell}j_{\ell}(kr)Y_{\ell m}^{*}(\bm{\hat{k}})Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\phi),

(4.6)

where
j_{\ell}
are the spherical Bessel functions and
Y_{\ell m}
are the (scalar) spherical harmonics.
This allows us to always expand the eigenmode as

\Upsilon^{E_{i}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})=4\pi\sum_{\ell m}j_{\ell}(kr)\xi^{E_{i};\bm{\hat{k}}}_{k\ell m}Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\phi).

(4.7)

For
E_{18}

\xi^{E_{18};\bm{\hat{k}}}_{k\ell m}=e^{-i\bm{k}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y_{\ell m}^{*}(\bm{\hat{k}}).

(4.8)

In certain cases, e.g., CMB fluctuations, observations project the scalar field
\delta^{X}
onto the sphere of the sky, integrating along the line of sight with an appropriate transfer function, so

\delta^{X}(\theta,\phi)=\sum_{\ell m}a^{E_{18};X}_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\phi),

(4.9)

where

\displaystyle a^{E_{18};X}_{\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int\mathrm{d}^{3}k~\delta^{\mathcal{R}}({\bm{k}})\xi^{E_{18};\bm{\hat{k}}}_{k\ell m}\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k).

(4.10)

Here
\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k)
is the spherical-harmonic transfer function from
\mathcal{R}
to
X
,
and which, relative to
\Delta^{X}(\bm{k})
absorbs the
j_{\ell}(kr)
that contributed to the integrand of the radial integral.
10
10

10

As with
\Delta^{X}(k)
,
\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k)
is typically only a function of the wavevector magnitude
k
, but in more generality we can have
\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(\bm{k})
dependent on the full three-dimensional wavevector.

If, as usual,
X
represents a real scalar quantity, then the spherical-harmonic coefficients satisfy
a_{\ell m}^{*}=(-1)^{m}a_{\ell-m}
and we only obtain unique physical information about the
a_{\ell m}
from
m\geq 0
.
In this paper, we will be particularly interested in the properties of the CMB temperature fluctuations, i.e., in
X=T
.

It is surprising, but easily proved, that the isotropy of
E_{18}
means that if the
\delta^{X}(\bm{k})
are independent Gaussian random variables of zero mean, with variance only dependent upon the magnitude
k
, then the coefficients
a^{X}_{\ell m}
are independent with variance only dependent upon
\ell
.
This leads to the customary statement of statistical isotropy,

C^{E_{18};XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\equiv\langle a^{X}_{\ell m}a^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\rangle=C^{XY}_{\ell}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{mm^{\prime}},

(4.11)

where
\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{ij}
is the Kronecker delta.

Non-trivial topological boundary conditions have two important effects on the eigenmodes of the Laplacian:

1.

Only certain wavevectors
\bm{k}
are “allowed” by the boundary conditions.
For the fully compact topologies
E_{1}
–
E_{10}
, the allowed wavevectors form a discrete lattice.
We write
\delta^{X}_{\bm{k}}
, not
\delta^{X}(\bm{k})
.
Thus the correlator
\langle\delta^{X}_{\bm{k}}\delta^{X*}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}\rangle
contains terms involving
{\mathcal{P}}^{X}(k)\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\prime}}
(i.e., a Kronecker, rather than Dirac, delta, although there can be a mix of the two for the chimney (
E_{11}
,
E_{12}
) and slab (
E_{16}
) spaces with a mix of finite and infinite directions).

2.

Except for
E_{1}
,
E_{11}
, and
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
, the eigenmodes are not single covering-space eigenmodes but instead linear combinations thereof, with different
\bm{k}
of the same magnitude. This induces extra terms in the correlator coupling
\bm{k}
to the generator’s rotations of
\bm{k}
with Kronecker or Dirac deltas.

Each of these effects encodes the violation of statistical isotropy,
and each of them breaks the surprising connection presented above between the statistics of
\delta^{X}(\bm{k})
and the statistics of
a^{X}_{\ell m}
.
Equations (
4.4
) and (
4.11
) no longer hold.
Instead of
C^{E_{18};XY}_{\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\prime}}\equiv\langle\delta^{X}(\bm{k})\delta^{Y*}(\bm{k}^{\prime})\rangle
being proportional to a Dirac delta function of
\bm{k}
and
\bm{k}^{\prime}
, it vanishes except for certain allowed
\bm{k}
and generically connects all pairs of allowed
\bm{k}
with correlations of equal magnitude and location-dependent phase.
Meanwhile,
\langle a^{X}_{\ell m}a^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\rangle
is also not diagonal:

\langle a^{X}_{\ell m}a^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\rangle=C^{XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}.

(4.12)

Despite the reality condition on the spherical-harmonic coefficients themselves, the quantity
C^{XY}_{\ell-m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}=\langle a^{X}_{\ell-m}a^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\rangle=(-1)^{m}\langle a^{X*}_{\ell m}a^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\rangle
does contain independent information. Rather, the
C^{XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}
matrix is Hermitian in the
(X,\ell m),(Y,\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime})
index sets.

In the subsections below, we present the eigenmodes and eigenspectra of the orientable Euclidean manifolds as functions of their topological parameters in their full generality.
Assuming that it is the amplitudes of these eigenmodes that are Gaussian random variables of zero mean and dimensionless power spectrum
{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)
, we present the correlation matrices for Fourier-mode amplitudes
C^{E_{i};XY}_{\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\prime}}
and spherical-harmonic amplitudes
C^{E_{i};XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}
.
The important results for each topology are boxed.
The generality of the results employs the orientation and other choices described in
Section

3
, but also includes both an arbitrary origin for the definition of the manifold parameters and an arbitrary location for the observer.
As discussed in
Section

3
, there are redundancies in these choices.
Any comprehensive search over parameters must take care to avoid overweighting some parts of parameter space.
In practice it is convenient to make one of two choices when employing the results below, either

1.

choose the observer to be at the origin,
\bm{x}_{0}=\bm{0}
, and use the “tilted” parameters of the manifold, or

2.

choose the “special” origin for the coordinate system, in which case the manifold parameters are simplified, but some of the components of observer location,
\bm{x}_{0}
, become significant.

4.1
General considerations for eigenmodes

In each of the manifolds, the eigenmodes of the scalar Laplacian
\Upsilon^{E_{i}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})
must be invariant under every possible group transformation
G_{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{E_{i}}
:

\Upsilon^{E_{i}}_{\bm{k}}(G_{\alpha}\bm{x})=\Upsilon^{E_{i}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})\,.

(4.13)

Formally, the solution is that
\Upsilon^{E_{i}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})
is a simple linear combination of all covering-space eigenmodes related by the group transformations

\Upsilon^{E_{i}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})\propto\sum_{G_{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{E_{i}}}e^{i\bm{k}\cdot G_{\alpha}\bm{x}}\,.

(4.14)

More practically, we can limit the sum to a small, finite set of group elements
G_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}}
,

\Upsilon^{E_{i}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N(\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}})}}\sum_{G_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}}}e^{i\bm{k}\cdot G_{\alpha}\bm{x}}\,,

(4.15)

where
N(\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}})
is the number of elements in
\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}}
.

\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}}
includes one group element for each of the
SO(3)
matrices
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{(G_{\alpha})}
that appears when we explicitly write the action of the group elements,

G_{\alpha}:(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})\to\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{(G_{\alpha})}(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})+\bm{v}^{(G_{\alpha})}\,.

(4.16)

These
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{(G_{\alpha})}
are then just the matrices
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{i}}_{a}
that appear in the generators, as described in
Section

2
, plus all non-identical
SO(3)
matrices that can be built from arbitrary products of those
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{i}}_{a}
.
Below, we will present the
\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}}
for each
E_{i}
.

Equation (
4.13
) must still be satisfied for every group element
G_{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{E_{i}}
.
Among those group elements are a subgroup of pure translations, which are all the integer linear combinations of the
\bm{T}^{E_{i}}_{j}
, i.e., the translations of the associated homogeneous space (
E_{1}
,
E_{11}
, or
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
) of that manifold.
Considering the invariance of
\Upsilon^{E_{i}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})
under the translation by
\bm{T}^{E_{i}}_{j}
, and recognizing that

\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}}
always includes the identity matrix, we learn that one must have

e^{i\bm{k}\cdot[(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})+\bm{T}^{E_{i}}_{j}]}=e^{i\bm{k}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}

(4.17)

or more compactly

\bm{k}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{i}}_{j}=2\pi n_{j}\,,\quad\mbox{for }n_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}.

(4.18)

This is exactly the discretization condition that we get with an
E_{1}
,
E_{11}
, or
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
.
In other words, the eigenmodes of the Laplacian on an
E_{i}
manifold are linear combinations of the Fourier modes that are eigenmodes of the associated homogeneous space.
For each
E_{i}
below, we present those discretization conditions.

Equation (
4.15
) satisfies the invariance condition (
4.13
) for all
\bm{k}
allowed by
(
4.18
), however in some cases the sum over
G_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{G}^{E_{i}}
yields more than one identical term.
This occurs when
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{(G_{\alpha})}\bm{k}=\bm{k}
for certain
\bm{k}
allowed by (
4.18
).
More specifically, for the manifolds in question, where
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{(G_{\alpha})}
are matrices
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{i}}_{a}
representing rotations by
2\pi p_{a}/q_{a}
(
p_{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
,
q_{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}
,
|p|
and
q
relatively prime) about one of the three coordinate axes, this occurs when
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{i}}_{a})^{N_{a}}\bm{k}=\bm{k}
has a solution for
N_{a}<q_{a}
. We will consider those cases explicitly for each
E_{i}
.

4.2
E_{1}
: 3-torus

The 3-torus is the simplest of the compact Euclidean topologies and will serve as a model for determining the eigenspectrum and eigenmodes of all the Euclidean three-manifolds.
In this subsection we determine which of the eigenvalues and eigenmodes of the scalar Laplacian acting on the covering space
E_{18}
are preserved by the isometries of the topology.
We then use that information to present the Fourier space and spherical-harmonic space correlation matrices of any fluctuations that are linearly related to independent Gaussian random fluctuations of the amplitudes of those eigenmodes.

We begin with the covering-space (
E_{18}
) eigenmodes (
4.1
).
Though in general the eigenmodes of
E_{i}
can be linear combinations of the
E_{18}
eigenmodes,
the
E_{1}
eigenmodes are the subset of the
E_{18}
eigenmodes that respect the
E_{1}
symmetries,

\Upsilon^{E_{1}}_{\bm{k}}(g^{E_{1}}_{A_{j}}\bm{x})=\Upsilon^{E_{1}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x}).

(4.19)

This is because all the group elements of
\Gamma^{E_{1}}
are pure translations, i.e.,

\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{(G_{\alpha})}=\mathds{1}
for all
G_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{G}^{E_{1}}
,
so
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{(G_{\alpha})}\bm{k}=\bm{k}
trivially.

As discussed above in general (cf. (
4.18
)),
the symmetry condition (
4.19
) leads to the discretization of the allowed
\bm{k}
in
E_{1}
:

\displaystyle 2\pi n_{1}

\displaystyle=(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}L_{1},

\displaystyle 2\pi n_{2}

\displaystyle=(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}L_{2}\cos\alpha+(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}L_{2}\sin\alpha,

(4.20)

\displaystyle 2\pi n_{3}

\displaystyle=(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}L_{3}\cos\beta\cos\gamma+(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}L_{3}\cos\beta\sin\gamma+(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}L_{3}\sin\beta.

Since the wavenumbers are now discretized they are labeled by integers
n_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
and we denote this explicitly by writing the wavevector as
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
for
\bm{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
.
Here and below we will use either the
n_{i}
or
(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{i}
labels as convenient for the situation.
Inverting these requirements, the components of the wavevectors are

\displaystyle(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}},

\displaystyle(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}\sin\alpha}-\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\frac{\cos\alpha}{\sin\alpha},

(4.21)

\displaystyle(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{L_{3}\sin\beta}-\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}}\frac{\cos\beta\sin\gamma}{\sin\alpha\sin\beta}-\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\frac{\cos\beta(\sin\alpha\cos\gamma-\cos\alpha\sin\gamma)}{\sin\alpha\sin\beta}.

Clearly the eigenvalues
k_{\bm{n}}=|\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}|
are complicated functions of
\bm{n}
.

Thus

\Upsilon^{E_{1}}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})},\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{1}},

(4.22)

where

\mathcal{N}^{E_{1}}\equiv\{(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})|n_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\}\setminus(0,0,0),

(4.23)

and

\xi^{E_{1};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\equiv e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y_{\ell m}^{*}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}).

(4.24)

Following (
4.4
) the Fourier-mode correlation matrix for
E_{1}
is

\displaystyle C^{E_{1};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle=V_{E_{1}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k_{\bm{n}}^{3}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}\;.

(4.25)

In transitioning from the covering space
E_{18}
we have replaced
(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime})
with
V_{E_{1}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}
, where the volume factor
V_{E_{1}}
is given by (
3.4
).

As for
E_{18}
above, we can project the field
\delta^{X}
onto the sky by performing a radial integral with suitable weight function and transfer function, giving

\displaystyle a^{E_{1};X}_{\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi}{V_{E_{1}}}\sum_{\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{1}}}\delta^{\mathcal{R}}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}}\xi^{E_{1};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k_{\bm{n}}).

(4.26)

Because
\mathcal{N}^{E_{1}}
labels only a discrete set of
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
, the integral over
\mathrm{d}^{3}k
in
Eq.

4.10
is replaced by a sum over
\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{1}}
.

For the compact topologies
E_{i}
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,6\}
, the harmonic space covariance matrix has the general form
11
11

11

Note that, while
\Delta^{Y}(\bm{k})
is complex,
\Delta^{Y}_{\ell}
is real for the usual cases of CMB temperature and polarization; nevertheless we retain the complex conjugate for generic
Y
.

C^{E_{i};XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}=\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{{V_{E_{i}}}}\sum_{\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{i}}}\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}}(k_{\bm{n}})\frac{2\pi^{2}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})}{k_{\bm{n}}^{3}}\xi^{E_{i};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\xi^{E_{i};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}*}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}.

(4.27)

4.3
E_{2}
: Half-turn space

The eigenspectrum and eigenmodes of the half-turn space can be determined in a manner analogous to that of the 3-torus.
We could begin from the covering space, but it is more expedient to recognize that
E_{2}
is
E_{1}
with extra symmetries imposed.
With this, the eigenspectrum of
E_{2}
will be discretized with wavevectors
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
and the eigenfunctions
\Upsilon^{E_{2}}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}}(\bm{x})
will be linear combinations of
\Upsilon^{E_{1}}_{\bm{k_{\bm{n}}}}(\bm{x})
.
For
E_{2}
the discretization condition (
4.18
) from the translation vectors
\bm{T}^{E_{2}}_{i}
leads to the components of the allowed wavevectors,

\displaystyle(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}},

\displaystyle(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}\sin\alpha}-\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\frac{\cos\alpha}{\sin\alpha},

(4.28)

\displaystyle(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{2L_{B}\sin\beta}.

Unlike in
E_{1}
, the eigenmodes of
E_{2}
can include a linear combination of two
E_{1}
eigenmodes.
This follows in the application of
Eq.

4.15
since the condition
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B})^{N}\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}=\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
has more than one solution for the minimum positive
N
, depending on
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
,
namely,
N=1
for
(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}=(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}=0
and
N=2
otherwise.
Written explicitly,

N=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={(0,0,(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z})}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(0,0,n_{3})
,

n_{3}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{2}}_{(0,0,n_{3})}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}=e^{i(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}(z-z_{0})},

(4.29)

N=2
eigenmodes:

((\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x},(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y})\neq(0,0)
,
i.e.,
(n_{1},n_{2})\neq(0,0)
, and per
Eq.

4.15
,

\Upsilon^{E_{2}}_{\bm{n}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}+e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})+\bm{T}^{E_{2}}_{B})}\right).

(4.30)

The linear combination in the
N=2
modes requires some care.
Notice that
{\bm{k}}^{T}_{\bm{n}}\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}=(-(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x},-(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y},(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z})
, i.e.,
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}
maps
(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\to(-n_{1},-n_{2},n_{3})
.
One implication of this is that summing over
(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
would double-count eigenmodes if all
n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and all
n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
were included.
Hence, we define two sets of allowed modes, one for
N=1
and another for
N=2
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{(0,0,n_{3})|n_{3}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\{(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})|n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{(0,n_{2},n_{3})|n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}\},

(4.31)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}_{1}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}_{2}.

With these the Fourier-mode correlation matrix can now be expressed as

\displaystyle C^{E_{2};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}={}

\displaystyle V_{E_{2}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}_{\bm{n}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})e^{i(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}-\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}\left[\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}_{1}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}}+{}\right.

(4.32)

\displaystyle\left.\quad{}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}_{2}}\sum_{a=0}^{1}\sum_{b=0}^{1}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{a}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{b}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})}\right],

where
V_{E_{2}}
is given in (
3.10
),
\bm{T}^{(0)}\equiv\bm{0}
, and
\bm{T}^{(1)}\equiv\bm{T}^{E_{2}}_{B}
.
12
12

12

In
Eq.

4.32
,
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
and
\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}
are wavevectors of the associated
E_{1}
, as specified above in (
4.3
).

C^{E_{2};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}
describes correlations between amplitudes of the plane waves that comprise the eigenmodes of a specific manifold — i.e., of a specific topology, with specific values of its parameters.
It is this object that would be used, for example, in creating realizations of initial conditions for large-scale structure simulations.
If one was, instead, constructing a likelihood function to compare data with expectations from
E_{2}
manifolds, one would need to convolve
C^{E_{2};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}
with a kernel characterizing the Fourier structure of the survey of interest.

Note that
C^{E_{2};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}=0
for
|k_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}|\neq|k_{\bm{n}}|
, so
{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})
,
\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})
, and
\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})
are each a function only of
k_{\bm{n}}
.

Another implication of the rotation in
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}
comes when representing the eigenmodes in the harmonic basis.
Here we will combine modes with the same eigenvalue
k_{\bm{n}}
and orientations
\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}
and
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}){}^{T}\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}
.
Since the half turn is a rotation around the
z
-axis by
\pi
we can use the rotation properties of the spherical harmonics to simplify our expressions.
In particular

Y^{*}_{\ell m}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}){}^{T}\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})=e^{im\pi}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})=(-1)^{m}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}).

(4.33)

This gives for the eigenmodes in the harmonic basis

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{2};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}),\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}_{1},

(4.34)

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{2};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\sum_{j=0}^{1}(-1)^{jm}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B})^{j}\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{2}}_{B}},\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{2}}_{2},

where it will prove useful for many of the topologies to define

\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{i}}_{00}\equiv\mathbb{0},\quad\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{01}\equiv\mathds{1},\quad\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{i}}_{0j}\equiv\sum_{r=0}^{j-1}(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{i}}_{B})^{r}.

(4.35)

Finally, the harmonic space covariance matrix has the form (
4.27
).

4.4
E_{3}
: Quarter-turn space

The eigenspectrum and eigenmodes of the quarter-turn space can be determined in a manner analogous to that for
E_{2}
.
For
E_{3}
the discretization condition (
4.18
) from the translation vectors
\bm{T}^{E_{3}}_{i}
leads to the components of the allowed wavevectors,

(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}=\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{A}},\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}=\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{A}},\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}=\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{4L_{B}\sin\beta}.

(4.36)

As in
E_{2}
, the eigenmodes of
E_{3}
can include linear combinations of
E_{1}
eigenmodes since
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B})^{N}\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}=\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
has more than one solution for the minimum positive
N
, depending on
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
.
Here we have

N=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={(0,0,(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z})}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(0,0,n_{3})
,

n_{3}\in 4\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{3}}_{(0,0,n_{3})}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})},

(4.37)

N=4
eigenmodes:

((\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x},(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y})\neq(0,0)
,
i.e.,
(n_{1},n_{2})\neq(0,0)
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{4}}_{\bm{n}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{4}}\sum_{j=0}^{3}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B})^{j}(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})+\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{3}}_{B})},

(4.38)

and
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{0j}
defined in (
4.35
).

As in
E_{2}
, the cyclic properties of
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B}
would lead to repeated counting of eigenmodes if all
n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
were included.
In this case, under the repeated action of
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B}
we have the mappings
(n_{1},n_{2})\to(n_{2},-n_{1})\to(-n_{1},-n_{2})\to(-n_{2},n_{1})
.
To avoid this, we define two sets of allowed modes, now for
N=1
and
N=4
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{(0,0,n_{3})|n_{3}\in 4\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\{(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})|n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}\},

(4.39)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}_{1}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}_{4}.

With these the Fourier-mode correlation matrix can now be expressed as

\displaystyle C^{E_{3};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}={}

\displaystyle V_{E_{3}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}_{\bm{n}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})e^{i(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}-\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}\left[\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}_{1}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}}+{}\right.

(4.40)

\displaystyle\left.\quad{}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}_{4}}\sum_{a=0}^{3}\sum_{b=0}^{3}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{a}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{b}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})}\right],

where
V_{E_{3}}
is given in (
3.15
),
\bm{T}^{(0)}\equiv\bm{0}
, and
\bm{T}^{(a)}\equiv\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{0a}\bm{T}^{E_{3}}_{B}
for
a\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Also as in
E_{2}
, we can use the rotation properties of the spherical harmonics along with the fact that
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B}
is a rotation around the
z
-axis by
\pi/2
to note that

Y^{*}_{\ell m}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{j}\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})=e^{imj\pi/2}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})=i^{mj}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}).

(4.41)

This gives for the eigenmodes in the harmonic basis

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{3};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}),\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}_{1},

(4.42)

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{3};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{4}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\sum_{j=0}^{3}i^{mj}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{B})^{j}\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{3}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{3}}_{B}},\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{3}}_{4},

and the harmonic space covariance matrix has the form (
4.27
).

4.5
E_{4}
: Third-turn space

The eigenspectrum and eigenmodes of the third-turn space can be determined in a manner analogous to those from above.
For
E_{4}
the discretization condition (
4.18
) from the translation vectors
\bm{T}^{E_{4}}_{i}
leads to the components of the allowed wavevectors,

(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}=\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{A}},\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}=\frac{2\pi}{\sqrt{3}L_{A}}(n_{1}+2n_{2}),\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}=\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{3L_{B}\sin\beta}.

(4.43)

As in
E_{2}
, the eigenmodes of
E_{4}
can include linear combinations of
E_{1}
eigenmodes since
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{B})^{N}\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}=\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
has more than one solution for the minimum positive
N
, depending on
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
.
Here we have

N=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={(0,0,(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z})}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(0,0,n_{3})
,

n_{3}\in 3\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{4}}_{(0,0,n_{3})}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})},

(4.44)

N=3
eigenmodes:

((\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x},(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y})\neq(0,0)
,
i.e.,
(n_{1},n_{2})\neq(0,0)
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{4}}_{\bm{n}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{B})^{j}(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})+\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{4}}_{B})},

(4.45)

and
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{0j}
defined in (
4.35
).

As in
E_{2}
, the cyclic properties of
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{B}
would lead to repeated counting of eigenmodes if all
n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
were included.
To avoid this, we define two sets of allowed modes, now for
N=1
and
N=3
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{(0,0,n_{3})|n_{3}\in 3\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}_{3}

\displaystyle=\{(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})|n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},n_{1}n_{2}\geq 0,n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}\},

(4.46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}_{1}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}_{3}.

With these the Fourier-mode correlation matrix can now be expressed as

\displaystyle C^{E_{4};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}={}

\displaystyle V_{E_{4}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}_{\bm{n}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})e^{i(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}-\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}\left[\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}_{1}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}}+{}\right.

(4.47)

\displaystyle\left.\quad{}+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}_{3}}\sum_{a=0}^{2}\sum_{b=0}^{2}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{a}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{b}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})}\right],

where
V_{E_{4}}
is given in (
3.21
),
\bm{T}^{(0)}\equiv\bm{0}
, and
\bm{T}^{(a)}\equiv\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{0a}\bm{T}^{E_{4}}_{B}
for
a\in\{1,2\}
.

Also as in
E_{2}
, we can use the rotation properties of the spherical harmonics along with the fact that
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{B}
is a rotation around the
z
-axis by
2\pi/3
to note that

Y^{*}_{\ell m}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{j}\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})=e^{imj2\pi/3}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}).

(4.48)

This gives for the eigenmodes in the harmonic basis

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{4};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}),\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}_{1},

(4.49)

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{4};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{imj2\pi/3}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{B})^{j}\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{4}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{4}}_{B}},\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{4}}_{3},

and the harmonic space covariance matrix has the form (
4.27
).

4.6
E_{5}
: Sixth-turn space

The eigenspectrum and eigenmodes of the sixth-turn space can be determined in a manner analogous to those from above, in particular it is very similar to
E_{4}
.
For
E_{5}
the discretization condition (
4.18
) from the translation vectors
\bm{T}^{E_{5}}_{i}
leads to the components of the allowed wavevectors,

(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}=\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{A}},\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}=\frac{2\pi}{\sqrt{3}L_{A}}(n_{1}+2n_{2}),\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}=\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{6L_{B}\sin\beta}.

(4.50)

As in
E_{2}
, the eigenmodes of
E_{5}
can include linear combinations of
E_{1}
eigenmodes since
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{B})^{N}\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}=\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
has more than one solution for the minimum positive
N
, depending on
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
.
Here we have

N=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={(0,0,(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z})}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(0,0,n_{3})
,

n_{3}\in 6\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{5}}_{(0,0,n_{3})}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})},

(4.51)

N=6
eigenmodes:

((\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x},(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y})\neq(0,0)
,
i.e.,
(n_{1},n_{2})\neq(0,0)
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{5}}_{\bm{n}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\sum_{j=0}^{5}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{B})^{j}(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})+\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{5}}_{B})},

(4.52)

and
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{0j}
defined in (
4.35
).

As in
E_{2}
, the cyclic properties of
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{B}
would lead to repeated counting of eigenmodes if all
n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
were included.
To avoid this, we define two sets of allowed modes, now for
N=1
and
N=6
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{(0,0,n_{3})|n_{3}\in 6\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}_{6}

\displaystyle=\{(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})|n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}\},

(4.53)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}_{1}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}_{6}.

With these the Fourier-mode correlation matrix can now be expressed as

\displaystyle C^{E_{5};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}={}

\displaystyle V_{E_{5}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}_{\bm{n}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})e^{i(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}-\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}\left[\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}_{1}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}}+{}\right.

(4.54)

\displaystyle\left.\quad{}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}_{6}}\sum_{a=0}^{5}\sum_{b=0}^{5}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{a}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{b}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})}\right],

where
V_{E_{5}}
is given in (
3.27
),
\bm{T}^{(0)}\equiv\bm{0}
, and
\bm{T}^{(a)}\equiv\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{0a}\bm{T}^{E_{5}}_{B}
for
a\in\{1,\ldots,5\}
.

Also as in
E_{2}
, we can use the rotation properties of the spherical harmonics along with the fact that
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{B}
is a rotation around the
z
-axis by
\pi/3
to note that

Y^{*}_{\ell m}([(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{j}\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})=e^{imj\pi/3}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}).

(4.55)

This gives for the eigenmodes in the harmonic basis

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{5};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}),\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}_{1},

(4.56)

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{5};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\sum_{j=0}^{5}e^{imj\pi/3}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{B})^{j}\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{5}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{5}}_{B}},\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{5}}_{6},

and the harmonic space covariance matrix has the form (
4.27
).

4.7
E_{6}
: Hantzsche-Wendt space

The eigenspectrum and eigenmodes of the Hantzsche-Wendt space can be determined in a manner analogous to those from above.
Complications arise from the fact that
E_{6}
contains rotations around multiple axes so a more careful discussion is warranted.
The discretization condition (
4.18
) from the translation vectors
\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{i}
is still straightforward and leads to the components of the allowed wavevectors,

(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}=\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{2L_{Ax}},\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}=\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{2L_{By}},\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}=\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{2L_{Cz}}.

(4.57)

As in
E_{2}
, the eigenmodes of
E_{6}
can include linear combinations of
E_{1}
eigenmodes since
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{a})^{N_{a}}\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}=\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
has more than one solution for the minimum positive
N_{a}
, depending on
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
, for
a\in\{A,B,C\}
.
Since all the rotations are half turns, i.e., all
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{a})^{2}=\mathds{1}
, there are linear combinations with the
N_{a}=1
and
N_{a}=2
.
At first glance
Eq.

4.15
seems to suggest that the eigenmodes with
N_{a}=1
will be a linear combination of four eigenmodes of
E_{1}
.
However, since
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{A}\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{B}=\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{C}
(and all permutations of
\{A,B,C\}
) along with the invariance of the eigenmodes under the group action (
4.13
) a linear combination of only two eigenmodes of
E_{1}
is required in this case.
Based on this we can choose

N_{A}=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={((\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x},0,0)}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(n_{1},0,0)
,

n_{1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{6}}_{(n_{1},0,0)}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}+e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{B}}\right],

(4.58)

N_{B}=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={(0,(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y},0)}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(0,n_{2},0)
,

n_{2}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{6}}_{(0,n_{2},0)}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}+e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{C}}\right],

(4.59)

N_{C}=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={((0,0,(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z})}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(0,0,n_{3})
,

n_{3}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{6}}_{(0,0,n_{3})}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}+e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{A}}\right],

(4.60)

N_{a}=2
eigenmodes:

at most one component of
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
zero, i.e., at most one of the
n_{i}
equal to zero, with

\Upsilon^{E_{6}}_{\bm{n}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{4}}\left[e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}+\sum_{a\in\{A,B,C\}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{a}(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{a}}\right].

(4.61)

The cyclic properties of the
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{a}
would lead to repeated counting of eigenmodes.
To avoid this, we define the sets of allowed modes as

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1A}

\displaystyle=\{(n_{1},0,0)|n_{1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1B}

\displaystyle=\{(0,n_{2},0)|n_{2}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1C}

\displaystyle=\{(0,0,n_{3})|n_{3}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\},

(4.62)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\{(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})|n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}\}

\displaystyle{}\qquad\cup\{(0,n_{2},n_{3})|n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}\}

\displaystyle{}\qquad\cup\{(n_{1},0,n_{3})|n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1A}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1B}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1C}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{2}.

With these the Fourier-mode correlation matrix can now be expressed as

\displaystyle C^{E_{6};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}={}

\displaystyle V_{E_{6}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}_{\bm{n}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})e^{i(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}-\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}\times{}

(4.63)

\displaystyle{}\quad\times\left[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1A}}\sum_{a,b\in\{0,B\}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{a}){}^{T}\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{b}){}^{T}\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})}+{}\right.

\displaystyle{}\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1B}}\sum_{a,b\in\{0,C\}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{a}){}^{T}\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{b}){}^{T}\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})}+{}

\displaystyle{}\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1C}}\sum_{a,b\in\{0,A\}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{a}){}^{T}\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{b}){}^{T}\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})}+{}

\displaystyle\left.\qquad{}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{2}}\sum_{a,b\in\{0,A,B,C\}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{a}){}^{T}\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{b}){}^{T}\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})}\right],

where
V_{E_{6}}
is given in (
3.33
),
\bm{T}^{(0)}\equiv\bm{0}
, and
\bm{T}^{(a)}\equiv\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{a}
for
a\in\{A,B,C\}
.

The rotation properties of the spherical harmonics can again be used to simplify the eigenmodes of
E_{6}
in the harmonic basis.
Though there are multiple axes of rotation, the fact that they are half turns allows by direct computation to show that

\displaystyle Y_{\ell m}^{*}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{A}){}^{T}\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell~-m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}),

\displaystyle Y_{\ell m}^{*}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{B}){}^{T}\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell+m}Y^{*}_{\ell~-m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}),

(4.64)

\displaystyle Y_{\ell m}^{*}((\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{C}){}^{T}\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}Y_{\ell m}^{*}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}).

With these the eigenmodes in the harmonic basis can be written in a number of useful forms.
We begin with general expressions patterned after the expressions given for
E_{1}
–
E_{5}
,

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{6};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\left[e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}+(-1)^{\ell}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{B}}\right],\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1A},

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{6};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\left[e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}+(-1)^{\ell}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{C}}\right],\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1B},

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{6};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\left[e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}+(-1)^{\ell}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{A}}\right],\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{1C},

(4.65)

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{6};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{4}}i^{\ell}\left[Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\left(1+(-1)^{m}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{C}\bm{x_{0}}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{C}}\right)+{}\right.

\displaystyle{}\qquad+(-1)^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell-m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\left(e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{A}\bm{x_{0}}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{A}}\right.+{}

\displaystyle{}\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{}+(-1)^{m}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{6}}_{B}\bm{x_{0}}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{T}^{E_{6}}_{B}}\right)\right],\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{6}}_{2},

and the harmonic space covariance matrix has the form (
4.27
).

4.8
E_{11}
: Chimney space

The chimney space is similar to the 3-torus though with only two compact dimensions and thus a finite “cross-sectional area” (
3.38
).
As in
E_{1}
, we begin with the covering-space (
E_{18}
) eigenmodes (
4.1
) and restrict ourselves to the set that respect the
E_{11}
symmetries,

\Upsilon^{E_{11}}_{\bm{k}}(g^{E_{11}}_{A_{j}}\bm{x})=\Upsilon^{E_{11}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x}).

(4.66)

Also as in
E_{1}
, since all the group elements of
\Gamma^{E_{11}}
are pure translations only one
E_{18}
eigenmode will contribute for each allowed
\bm{k}
.
The two generators of
E_{11}
(
3.7
) lead to two (rather than three as in
E_{1}
) discretization conditions following (
4.18
),

\displaystyle 2\pi n_{1}

\displaystyle=(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}L_{1},

(4.67)

\displaystyle 2\pi n_{2}

\displaystyle=(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}L_{2}\cos\alpha+(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}L_{2}\sin\alpha.

These can be inverted to determine the components of the allowed wavevectors,

\displaystyle(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}},

(4.68)

\displaystyle(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}\sin\alpha}-\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\frac{\cos\alpha}{\sin\alpha},

while
(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}\equiv k_{z}
is unconstrained.
We will write
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
as a shorthand for the wavevector parametrized by
(n_{1},n_{2};k_{z})
, i.e., by the integers
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
and the real variable
k_{z}
.
The usual (untilted) results are recovered for
\alpha=\pi/2
.

Thus

\Upsilon^{E_{11}}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}\,,\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{11}}\,,

(4.69)

where

\mathcal{N}^{E_{11}}\equiv\{(n_{1},n_{2})|n_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\}\,,

(4.70)

and

\xi^{E_{11};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\equiv e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y_{\ell m}^{*}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\,.

(4.71)

Following (
4.4
) the Fourier-mode correlation matrix for
E_{11}
is

\displaystyle C^{E_{11};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle=2\pi A_{E_{11}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k_{\bm{n}}^{3}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}})_{x}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}})_{y}}\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k_{z}-k^{\prime}_{z})\,.

(4.72)

In transitioning from
E_{1}
(compare
Eq.

4.72
to
Eq.

4.25
) we have replaced
V_{E_{1}}
with
2\pi A_{E_{11}}
(the cross-sectional area given by (
3.38
)) and
\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}
by a Kronecker delta for the
x
and
y
components of
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
and a Dirac delta function for the
z
component.

We can project the field
\delta^{X}
onto the sky by performing a radial integral with a suitable weight function and transfer function, giving

\displaystyle a^{E_{11};X}_{\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi}{2\pi A_{E_{11}}}\sum_{(n_{1},n_{2})\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{11}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{z}\,\delta^{\mathcal{R}}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}}\xi^{E_{11};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k_{\bm{n}}),

(4.73)

where a similar transition to that above was performing in starting from
Eq.

4.26
and replacing the sum over the
(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}
with the integral over
k_{z}
.

For the chimney spaces
E_{i}
with
i=11
or
i=12
, the harmonic space covariance matrix now has the form

C^{E_{i};XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}=\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{{2\pi A_{E_{i}}}}\sum_{(n_{1},n_{2})\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{i}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{z}\,\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}}(k_{\bm{n}})\frac{2\pi^{2}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})}{k_{\bm{n}}^{3}}\xi^{E_{i};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\xi^{E_{i};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}*}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}.

(4.74)

4.9
E_{12}
: Chimney space with half turn

The eigenspectrum and eigenmodes of the chimney space with half turn can be determined in a manner analogous to that of the chimney space in much the same way that these quantities for
E_{2}
were determined from
E_{1}
.
An important difference is that
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{2}}_{B}
is a rotation around the
z
-axis (
3.2
), whereas
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{12}}_{B}
is a rotation around the
y
-axis (
3.8
).
For
E_{12}
the discretization condition (
4.18
) leads to the components of the allowed wavevectors,

(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}=\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{Ax}},\quad(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}=\frac{\pi n_{2}}{L_{By}},

(4.75)

with
k_{z}
again unconstrained.
As in
E_{11}
, we will write
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
as a shorthand for the wavevector parametrized by the integer array
\bm{n}=(n_{1},n_{2})
and the real variable
k_{z}
.

Unlike in
E_{11}
, the eigenmodes of
E_{12}
can include a linear combination of two
E_{11}
eigenmodes.
Here
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{12}}_{B})^{N}\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}=\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
has two solutions,
N=1
and
N=2
.
Written explicitly,

N=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={(0,(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y},k_{z})}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(0,n_{2};0)
,

n_{2}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
,
k_{z}=0
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{12}}_{(0,n_{2};0)}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}=e^{i\pi n_{2}(y-y_{0})/L_{By}},

(4.76)

N=2
eigenmodes:

((\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x},(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y})\neq(0,0)
,
i.e.,
(n_{1},n_{2})\neq(0,0)
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{12}}_{\bm{n}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}+e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{12}}_{B}(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})+\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B})}\right).

(4.77)

Here the two sets of allowed modes are defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv\{(0,n_{2})|n_{2}\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv\{(n_{1},n_{2})|n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\},

(4.78)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}_{1}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}_{2}\,.

With these the Fourier-mode correlation matrix can now be expressed as

\displaystyle C^{E_{12};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}={}

\displaystyle 2\pi A_{E_{12}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}_{\bm{n}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})e^{i(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}-\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}\times{}

\displaystyle{}\;\times\frac{1}{2}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(\tilde{n}_{1},\tilde{n}_{2})\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\tilde{k}_{z}\,\sum_{a=0}^{1}\sum_{b=0}^{1}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}(\bm{k}^{(a)}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})_{x}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}})_{x}(\bm{k}^{(b)}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})_{x}}

(4.79)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}(\bm{k}^{(a)}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})_{y}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}})_{y}(\bm{k}^{(b)}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}})_{y}}\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k_{z}-\tilde{k}^{(a)}_{z})\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k^{\prime}_{z}-\tilde{k}^{(b)}_{z}),

where the terms with
\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}_{1}
are of measure zero and have been dropped,
A_{E_{12}}
is given in (
3.43
),
\bm{T}^{(0)}\equiv\bm{0}
,
\bm{T}^{(1)}\equiv\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}
,
\bm{k}^{(a)}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}}\equiv[(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{12}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{a}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}
, and
\tilde{k}^{(a)}_{z}\equiv(\bm{k}^{(a)}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})_{z}
.

In the harmonic basis we have

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{12};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}),\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}_{1}\,,

(4.80)

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{12};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\sum_{j=0}^{1}i^{mj}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{12}}_{B})^{j}\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{12}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{12}}_{B}}\,,\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{12}}_{2},

and the harmonic space covariance matrix has the form (
4.74
).

4.10
E_{16}
: Slab space including rotation

As discussed above (
Section

3.9
), there are two physically distinct cases for
E_{16}
which will be discussed separately.

4.10.1
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
: Conventional unrotated slab space

Similar to
E_{1}
and
E_{11}
,
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
is homogeneous.
It is compact in only one dimension so there is only one generator of the topology.
The one discretization condition following from (
4.18
) leads to

(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}=\frac{2\pi n}{L}\,,

(4.81)

while
(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}\equiv k_{x}
and
(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}\equiv k_{y}
are unconstrained.
As in the chimney spaces, we will again write
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
as a shorthand for the wavevector characterized by the integer
n
and the real variables
k_{x}
and
k_{y}
.

Thus

\Upsilon^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})},

(4.82)

where

\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}}=\{n\in\mathbb{Z}\},

(4.83)

and

\xi_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}\equiv e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x_{0}}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\,,\quad{\bm{n}}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}}.

(4.84)

Following (
4.4
) the Fourier-mode correlation matrix for
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
is

\displaystyle C^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{2}L\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})\times{}

(4.85)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k_{x}-k^{\prime}_{x})\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k_{y}-k^{\prime}_{y})\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}})_{z}}.

In transitioning from
E_{1}
(compare
Eq.

4.85
to
Eq.

4.25
) we have replaced
V_{E_{1}}
with
(2\pi)^{2}L
and
\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}
by a Kronecker delta for the
z
component of
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
and Dirac delta functions for the
x
and
y
components.

We can project the field
\delta^{X}
onto the sky by performing a radial integral with a suitable weight function and transfer function, giving

a^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})};X}_{\ell m}=\frac{4\pi}{(2\pi)^{2}L}\sum_{n\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{x}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{y}\,\delta^{\mathcal{R}}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}}\xi^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k_{\bm{n}}),

(4.86)

where a similar transition to that above was performing in starting from
Eq.

4.26
.

For the slab spaces the harmonic space covariance matrix now has the form

C^{E_{16}^{(a)};XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}=\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{(2\pi)^{2}L}\sum_{n\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(a)}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{x}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{y}\,\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}}(k_{\bm{n}})\frac{2\pi^{2}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)}{k_{\bm{n}}^{3}}\xi^{E_{16}^{(a)};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\xi^{E_{16}^{(a)};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}*}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\,,

(4.87)

where
a\in\{\mathrm{h},\mathrm{i}\}
labels the homogeneous (
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
) and inhomogeneous (
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
) slab spaces.

4.10.2
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
: General rotated slab space

The eigenspectrum and eigenmodes of
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
can be determined from
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
in a manner similar to that for
E_{12}
from
E_{11}
since
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B}
is a rotation, though now around the
z
-axis.
For
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
the discretization condition (
4.18
) leads to the component of the allowed wavevectors,

(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}=\frac{2\pi n}{qL\sin\beta},

(4.88)

while
(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{x}\equiv k_{x}
and
(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{y}\equiv k_{y}
are again unconstrained.
As in
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
, we will again write
\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
as a shorthand for the wavevector characterized by the integer
n
and the real variables
k_{x}
and
k_{y}
.

Unlike in
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
, the eigenmodes of
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
can include linear combinations of
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
eigenmodes.
Here
(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B})^{N}\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}=\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}
has two solutions,
N=1
and
N=q
. Written explicitly,

N=1
eigenmodes:

\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}={(0,0,(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z})}^{T}
,
i.e.,
\bm{n}=(0,0;n)
,

n\in q\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{(0,0;n)}(\bm{x})=e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})},

(4.89)

N=q
eigenmodes:

(k_{x},k_{y})\neq(0,0)
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, with

\Upsilon^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{\bm{n}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B})^{j}(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B}},

(4.90)

and
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{00}\equiv 0
and
\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{0j}
defined in (
4.35
).
Here the two sets of allowed modes are defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{n\in q\mathbb{Z}^{\neq 0}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{q}

\displaystyle=\{n\in\mathbb{Z}\},

(4.91)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{1}\cup\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{q}.

With these the Fourier-mode correlation matrix can now be expressed as

\displaystyle C^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})};XY}_{\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{2}L_{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k_{\bm{n}}^{3}}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{X}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{Y*}(k_{\bm{n}})e^{i(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}}-\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}\times{}

(4.92)

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\frac{1}{q}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\tilde{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{q}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{x}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{y}\,\sum_{a=0}^{q-1}\sum_{b=0}^{q-1}e^{i\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}}\cdot(\bm{T}^{(a)}-\bm{T}^{(b)})}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\bm{n}})_{z}(\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}}^{(a)})_{z}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{(\bm{k}_{\smash{\bm{n}^{\prime}}})_{z}(\bm{k}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}}^{(b)})_{z}}\times{}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\times\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k_{x}-\tilde{k}^{(a)}_{x})\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k^{\prime}_{x}-\tilde{k}^{(b)}_{x})\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k_{y}-\tilde{k}^{(a)}_{y})\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(k^{\prime}_{y}-\tilde{k}^{(b)}_{y}),

where the terms with
n\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{1}
are of measure zero and have been dropped,
L_{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}
is given in (
3.47
),
\bm{T}^{(0)}\equiv\bm{0}
,
\bm{T}^{(a)}\equiv\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{0a}\bm{T}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B}
for
a\in\{1,\ldots,q-1\}
,
\bm{k}^{(a)}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}}\equiv[(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B}){}^{T}]^{a}\bm{k}_{\bm{\tilde{n}}}
, and
\tilde{k}^{(a)}_{w}\equiv(\bm{k}^{(a)}_{\tilde{\bm{n}}})_{w}
for
w\in\{x,y\}
.

In the harmonic basis we have

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}),\quad\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{1},

(4.93)

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{q}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}})\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}e^{i\pi mjp/q}e^{-i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot(\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B})^{j}\bm{x}_{0}}e^{i\bm{k}_{\bm{n}}\cdot\bm{\mathsf{M}}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{0j}\bm{T}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{B}},\;\mbox{for }\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}}_{q},

and the harmonic space covariance matrix has the form (
4.87
).

4.11
E_{18}
: The covering space,
E^{3}

The covering space itself has all of the usual Fourier and spherical-harmonic mode functions, along with a simple isotropic correlation structure. The eigenmodes are

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{E_{18}}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=e^{i\bm{k}\cdot(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0})}\,,

(4.94)

the Fourier-mode correlation matrix is

\displaystyle C^{E_{18};XY}_{\bm{k}\bm{k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)\Delta^{X}(k)\Delta^{Y*}(k)\delta^{(\textrm{D})}(\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime}),

(4.95)

and the harmonic space covariance matrix has the form

\displaystyle C^{E_{18};XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi}\int\mathrm{d}^{3}\bm{k}\,\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(\bm{k})\Delta^{Y*}_{\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{k})\frac{2\pi^{2}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)}{k^{3}}\xi_{\ell m}^{E_{18};\bm{k}}\xi_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}^{E_{18};\bm{k}*}

(4.96)

with

\displaystyle\xi^{E_{18};\bm{k}}_{\ell m}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\bm{k}\cdot\bm{x}_{0}}i^{\ell}Y^{*}_{\ell m}(\bm{\hat{k}}).

(4.97)

In the typical case (e.g., CMB temperature and polarization) where
\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(\bm{k})=\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k)
,
Eq.

4.96
gives the isotropic
C^{E_{18};XY}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}=C^{E_{18};XY}_{\ell}\delta_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}
, with the angular power spectrum given by

C^{E_{18};XY}_{\ell}=4\pi\int\frac{\mathrm{d}k}{k}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k)\Delta^{X}_{\ell}(k)\Delta^{Y*}_{\ell}(k)\;.

(4.98)

5
Numerical analysis

As described above, to the extent that the CMB is Gaussian, all the information about the temperature anisotropies is given by the 2-point correlation matrix of the spherical-harmonic coefficients, namely the covariance matrix
C_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}^{E_{i};XY}=\langle a_{\ell m}^{E_{i};X}a_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}^{E_{i};Y*}\rangle

[
79
]
.
In the familiar case of the isotropic covering space, we have the usual formula
\langle a_{\ell m}^{E_{18};X}a_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}^{E_{18};Y*}\rangle=C_{\ell}^{E_{18};XY}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\delta^{(\textrm{K})}_{mm^{\prime}}
, where only the diagonal elements are non-zero and they are independent of
m
.
But a non-trivial topology breaks the assumption of isotropy (see, e.g., Ref.
[
24
]
), inducing non-zero values for off-diagonal components of the correlation matrix.
In this section, we numerically compute the correlation matrix elements for sample manifolds of each of the compact, orientable, Euclidean topologies
E_{1}
–
E_{6}
.
(The non-compact cases
E_{11}
,
E_{12}
, and
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{h})}
can be regarded as limiting cases of
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
, and
E_{16}
, respectively.
We reserve detailed investigation of
E_{16}^{(\mathrm{i})}
for future specific work.)
We then estimate the detectability of this off-diagonal signal of topology given the cosmic variance associated with the primary CMB anisotropies.

5.1
Evaluation of the CMB temperature covariance matrices

The covariance matrices for the fully compact, orientable, Euclidean topologies can generally be written like Eq. (
4.27
), which we rewrite here for auto-correlation of the CMB temperature (T) fluctuations:

\displaystyle C^{E_{i};\,\mathrm{TT}}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}=\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{{V_{E_{i}}}}\sum_{\bm{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{E_{i}}}\Delta^{\mathrm{T}}_{\ell}(k_{\bm{n}})\Delta^{\mathrm{T}}_{\ell^{\prime}}(k_{\bm{n}})\frac{2\pi^{2}\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\bm{n}})}{k_{\bm{n}}^{3}}\xi^{E_{i};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell m}\xi^{E_{i};\bm{\hat{k}}_{\bm{n}}*}_{k_{\bm{n}}\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}.

(5.1)

For brevity, we will drop the
\mathrm{T}
and
\mathrm{TT}
labels in this section. The covering space,
E_{18}
, reduces this three-dimensional summation to a one-dimensional integral over the magnitude of the wavenumber
|\bm{k}|
(or
k
), which is computationally fast to evaluate numerically.
However, for non-trivial topologies, we must perform computationally costly summations.
In principle, we need to perform the sum over an infinite set of wavenumbers, but in practice, we must choose a maximum value of
|\bm{k}|
,
|\bm{k}_{\textrm{max}}(\ell)|
, at which we stop the summation.
As we are interested in probing Dirichlet domains that fully contain the last scattering surface, we are forced to prioritize large topology scales (heuristically, the various
L_{i}
) over the computational accuracy that might be achieved by calculating the summation up to a large
|\bm{k}|
.

As an example, consider the cubic
E_{1}
, the 3-torus with equal lengths
L_{A_{1}}=L_{A_{2}}=L_{A_{3}}=L
.
Here, the wavevector is

\bm{k}=\frac{2\pi}{L}\bm{n}.

(5.2)

In making
L
larger, we are forced to go to larger values of
|\bm{n}|
to achieve a given accuracy for a given element of
C^{E_{1}}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}
,
meaning we must execute a larger number of summations.
Since we live in three spatial dimensions, the number of operations we must perform for a given
\{\ell,m,\ell^{\prime},m^{\prime}\}
scales as
\mathcal{O}(L^{3})
. Therefore, we need to make a clear definition for our multipole-dependent cutoff
|\bm{k}_{\textrm{max}}(\ell)|
. We define the ratio function

R_{\ell}(|\bm{k}|)=\frac{C^{|\bm{k}|}_{\ell}}{C^{\Lambda\textrm{CDM}}_{\ell}},

(5.3)

where

C^{|\bm{k}|}_{\ell}=4\pi\int^{|\bm{k}|}_{0}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}\,\frac{\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime})}{k^{\prime}}\Delta_{\ell}(k^{\prime})^{2},

(5.4)

and
C^{\Lambda\textrm{CDM}}_{\ell}
is the standard
\Lambda
CDM angular power spectrum produced by
CAMB

[
80
,
81
]
.
We then find
|\bm{k}_{\textrm{max}}(\ell)|
such that
R_{\ell}(|\bm{k}_{\textrm{max}}(\ell)|)=0.99
.
In other words, we take the discrete sum over wavevectors that would contribute to 99% of the angular power spectrum if the Universe had the trivial topology.
In the limit of
L\rightarrow\infty
, we would find a power spectrum that is 1% smaller than the actual power spectrum.
For computing the off-diagonal elements
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
, we perform the sum in
Eq.

5.1
up to
|\bm{k}_{\textrm{max}}(\max(\ell,\ell^{\prime}))|
.
Increasing the precision to a higher number than
99\%
has a negligible impact on the KL divergence we define later.

Figure 3
:
Effect of rotating the coordinate system on the CMB temperature covariance matrix.
The left panel shows the absolute value of the rescaled covariance matrix
\Xi^{E_{i};\,\mathrm{TT}}_{\ell m\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}
(at low multipoles
\ell
) in the coordinate system used for this work as opposed to a random rotation of the coordinate system in the right panel.
Here, the topology is the untilted
E_{2}
with
L_{A}=1.4L_{\mathrm{LSS}}
and
L_{B}=L_{\mathrm{LSS}}
, and with an on-axis observer.
For each
(\ell,\ell^{\prime})
block the matrix elements show the corresponding
(m,m^{\prime})
in increasing order, i.e.,
-\ell\leq m\leq\ell
.
